Signature Improvement Maturity: Universal Improvement Generator

JAH-UIGS-000022 · Improvement Maturity

Problem: The problem this design addresses is the absence of a worked design for Improvement Maturity inside the Universal Improvement Generator. A design-study answers that absence with a proposal that can be read, criticized, and improved, without pretending the proposal is a finished system. The purpose of this database is to record how a system is improved under control, so that change makes things better instead of breaking what already works. Improvement without control is guessing: a change is made, something else breaks, and no one can say which change caused it or how to undo it. Controlled improvement measures the baseline first, designs one change at a time, tries it in a controlled trial, compares the result against the baseline, keeps the change only if it is measurably better, and can roll it back if it is not. Every record here specifies one part of that workflow completely enough that an improver could follow it, a builder could implement it, and a checker could test whether the improvement was real or imagined. As a design, it is judged on whether it is complete, coherent, and testable, not on whether it has been built.

Method: This Signature design-study proposes a design for Improvement Maturity as one working part of the Universal Improvement Generator. Its working angle is risks. Its risks are written down before work starts: what can go wrong, what the failure would look like, and what safe state the work falls back to, so a failure is a handled event, not a surprise. All values in this design are illustrative design values, never presented as measured results or as a built system. Scope, as a design: The scope covers controlled improvement: improvement proposals, baseline measurement, change design, controlled trials, comparison against baseline, rollback plans, improvement records, regression checks, performance tracking, cost-benefit review, stakeholder review, gradual rollout, the improvement backlog, root-cause analysis, small-step changes, measurement discipline, improvement metrics, knowledge capture, improvement governance, automation of improvement, cross-system improvement, improvement maturity, the improvement registry, and the improvement lifecycle. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being improved, which belong to their own databases, and any claim that a particular improvement succeeded, which only its own trial record can make. This database records the workflow; it does not duplicate the results. Requirements, as design requirements: Every improvement workflow in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must measure the baseline before any change, so improvement can be compared against something real. Second, it must change one thing at a time, so the cause of any effect is known. Third, it must run a controlled trial, with the same tests before and after the change. Fourth, it must compare the result against the baseline with stated numbers, not with impressions. Fifth, it must keep a rollback plan that has itself been tested, so a bad change can be undone. Sixth, it must record every trial, kept or rejected, so failed improvements are not retried blindly. Seventh, it must be written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces, as designed: Controlled improvement connects through four interfaces. The baseline interface records the measured state of the system before change, with the tests and the numbers. The proposal interface accepts one change at a time, with its design, its expected effect, and its rollback plan. The trial interface runs the same tests before and after the change and writes both results to the trial record. The registry interface lists every improvement with its verdict, kept or rolled back, so the Master AI can see the history of what was tried and what worked. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, and every trial is stored, so an improvement decision can be audited after the fact. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this design-study is a complete proposed design for Improvement Maturity, with acceptance tests that say how the design would be judged if it were built. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph, applied as design criteria. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This design applies whenever someone must propose, review, or teach Improvement Maturity inside the Universal Improvement Generator: the designer follows the method, the reviewer runs the acceptance tests as design criteria, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full design. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the live continuous-system-improvement pair, with 34 records in each edition, and the live self-improvement-experiment-database pair, with 25 records in each edition, which are subject databases about improvement, not a generator running controlled improvement workflows under the paste's section 6 standard and section 7 lifecycle. It also links the Foundation program's staged automated-testing-generator and meta-generator pairs, package-only, and the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, whose self-improvement cycle carries the core rule that generated modifications cannot automatically overwrite the trusted runtime without validation and approval.

Verification path: Re-read the catalog item #96 and the cross-check row for it; confirm the slug and prefix are the pair's own; run this record's demo and confirm Deterministic: MATCH; confirm the design-study label, the maturity label, and the implementation status appear in the record; confirm the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions appear in plain words; confirm every cited source returns HTTP 200.

Each record carries the same boundless generator: it makes full records on demand; generated content is labeled simulated for weeding and double-checked.

7. AI

The AI of this database is JAH Universal Improvement Librarian (Signature). It explains this database in plain, conversational language, answers questions about any of the 24 records, and can create an AI assistant exactly like itself. It answers from this database's own data only, and says plainly when a question goes beyond it.

  • ai-thing.json — the per-database AI spec (the database's own AI, JAH Universal Improvement Librarian (Signature)).
  • ai-discussion.json — the discussion AI spec, built to discuss The Signature Universal Improvement Generator Database with a visitor, record by record.

Both are free downloads, free forever, no sign-in.

8. Demo

Every record in this database carries a working demo program and a boundless generator. The demo for this website is demo.js — run it with node demo.js. It searches the edition's real 24 records and runs a deterministic generator demo (seed 7) over them. The run output is saved in demo-test.txt and shown below, exactly as executed — this demo actually ran.

DATABASE: universal-improvement-generator-signature
EDITION: signature
RECORDS: 24
SEARCH "signature": 24 hit(s)
  - JAH-UIGS-000001 :: Signature Improvement Proposals: Universal Improvement Generator
  - JAH-UIGS-000002 :: Signature Baseline Measurement: Universal Improvement Generator
  - JAH-UIGS-000003 :: Signature Change Design: Universal Improvement Generator
  - JAH-UIGS-000004 :: Signature Controlled Trials: Universal Improvement Generator
  - JAH-UIGS-000005 :: Signature Comparison Against Baseline: Universal Improvement Generator
SEARCH "" (all): 24 record(s)
GENERATOR DEMO (seed 7, 5 samples):
  GEN-1: Signature Proposals
